    </tr>
  </tbody>
</table>
</div><p>The example alpha is deliberately simple. Its Spearman IC averages 0.012 with an
ICIR of 0.16 (t-stat 3.97) and a hit rate of 56%: a genuine but modest
predictive signal, in line with typical daily alpha signals. The simple
portfolios earn an annualized information ratio above 1.7 with substantial
turnover, gross of any trading friction. Whether such a signal can be monetized
depends on transaction costs, borrow costs, market impact and turnover
constraints, which enter at the optimization step and are covered in
<a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-portfolio-construction"><span class="std std-ref">Portfolio Construction</span></a>.</p>
<p>All diagnostics are computed on the final alpha forecast returned by the
estimator, after any rank transformation. With
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">scoring_transformer=CSGaussianRankScaler()</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">spearman_ic</span></code> measures the ordering
quality of the forecast and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">pearson_ic</span></code> the linear relation between
Gaussian-rank scores and future target returns. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">zscore_weighted_portfolio</span></code> is
the simple portfolio closest to the expected-return vector consumed by an
optimizer, since the optimizer receives alpha values proportional to those
scores.</p>
<p>The <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AlphaForecastEvaluation</span></code></a> result groups the diagnostics
as follows:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.ic_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.ic_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ic_summary</span></code></a>: <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">spearman_ic</span></code>
measures ordering quality and is invariant to monotonic transformations of the
forecast. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">pearson_ic</span></code>, weighted by <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">cs_weighting</span></code>, measures whether forecast
magnitudes are linearly related to realized targets.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.portfolio_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.portfolio_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">portfolio_summary</span></code></a>: annualized
statistics of alpha-only, gross-normalized long-short portfolios built from
centered ranks (<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">rank_weighted_portfolio</span></code>) or centered forecast values
(<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">zscore_weighted_portfolio</span></code>), before covariance, costs and constraints are
introduced by the optimizer.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.quantile_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.quantile_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">quantile_summary</span></code></a>: annualized
top-minus-bottom target returns per tail quantile, showing whether predictive
content is concentrated in the tails.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.calibration_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.calibration_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">calibration_summary</span></code></a>:
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">calibration_slope</span></code> is the scale multiplier from a weighted zero-intercept
regression of realized target on forecast. A slope near 1.0 indicates the
forecast is already scaled to target units. Values above 1.0 indicate that
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">forecast_scale</span></code> is too small, while values below 1.0 indicate that it is too
large.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.coverage_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.coverage_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">coverage_summary</span></code></a>: fraction and
number of assets with finite forecast and target values. Low coverage can make
the other statistics unstable even when their averages look acceptable.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.factor_correlation_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.factor_correlation_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">factor_correlation_summary</span></code></a>:
contemporaneous cross-sectional correlation between the forecast and factor
exposures, testing whether the forecast is neutral to existing factors.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.decay_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.decay_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">decay_summary</span></code></a> and
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.holding_period_summary" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.holding_period_summary"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">holding_period_summary</span></code></a>: IC and
simple-portfolio statistics across forward periods, showing whether the signal
is short-lived, persistent or delayed relative to the selected holding horizon.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Together, these diagnostics separate ordering quality from magnitude quality:
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">spearman_ic</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">rank_weighted_portfolio</span></code> isolate the ordering, while
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">pearson_ic</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">zscore_weighted_portfolio</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">calibration_summary</span></code> evaluate the
forecast values that an optimizer receives. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">coverage_summary</span></code> checks data
availability and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">decay_summary</span></code> aligns the alpha horizon with the intended
rebalancing and holding period. When the rank-based diagnostics are stronger
than the magnitude-based ones, the optimizer should not receive raw alpha
magnitudes as if they were calibrated expected returns.</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">evaluation</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_cumulative_ic</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/alpha_eval_cumulative_ic.webp"
       alt="Cumulative Pearson and Spearman information coefficients for the alpha forecast over time"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="eager" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">evaluation</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_factor_correlation</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/alpha_eval_factor_correlation.webp"
       alt="Correlation of the alpha forecast with market and style factor exposures"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">evaluation</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_cumulative_returns</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/alpha_eval_cumulative_returns.webp"
       alt="Cumulative returns of alpha-sorted long-short portfolios across holding periods"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><p>Both ICs accumulate steadily across the sample, with the strongest run in
2020-2021 and no prolonged negative stretch, indicating stable predictive power
rather than a few favorable periods. The factor correlation plot shows the
forecast is not fully factor-neutral as it carries a positive size correlation and
negative profitability and liquidity correlations, inherited from its
descriptors. If these tilts are unwanted, they can be removed with
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">neutralize_against</span></code>. The simple portfolios compound consistently, with a sharp
drawdown and recovery around the COVID shock.</p>
<p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AlphaForecastEvaluation</span></code></a> provides additional plots:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_rolling_ic" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_rolling_ic"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_rolling_ic</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_quantile_returns" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_quantile_returns"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_quantile_returns</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_calibration" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_calibration"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_calibration</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_ic_by_holding_period" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_ic_by_holding_period"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_ic_by_holding_period</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_portfolio_by_holding_period" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_portfolio_by_holding_period"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_portfolio_by_holding_period</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_ic_decay" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_ic_decay"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_ic_decay</span></code></a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.html#skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_portfolio_decay" title="skfolio.alpha.AlphaForecastEvaluation.plot_portfolio_decay"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_portfolio_decay</span></code></a></p></li>
</ul>
<p>When the diagnostics show reliable ordering but weak magnitude calibration, a
practical approach is to keep the alpha shape rank-based and control its strength
with a single scale parameter. For descriptor-composition estimators, this is
done with a rank-based <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">scoring_transformer</span></code> (e.g.
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.preprocessing.CSGaussianRankScaler.html#skfolio.preprocessing.CSGaussianRankScaler" title="skfolio.preprocessing.CSGaussianRankScaler"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">CSGaussianRankScaler</span></code></a>), as in the
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.alpha.FixedWeightedAlpha.html#skfolio.alpha.FixedWeightedAlpha" title="skfolio.alpha.FixedWeightedAlpha"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">FixedWeightedAlpha</span></code></a> example of the
<a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-alpha"><span class="std std-ref">Alpha Estimators</span></a> section. The resulting alpha is
still in expected-return units:</p>
<div class="math notranslate nohighlight">
\[\alpha_i = s \, z_i\]</div>
<p>where <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(z_i\)</span> is the cross-sectional Gaussian rank score and <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(s\)</span> is
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">forecast_scale</span></code>. The rank transformation defines the relative shape of the forecast,
while <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">forecast_scale</span></code> maps one rank-normal score unit to expected-return units.</p>
<p>This unit conversion is important in mean-risk optimization as the optimizer trades off
expected return, risk, constraints and transaction costs in the same objective. If
transaction costs are expressed in return units, the alpha forecast must also be in
return units. A rank-based alpha satisfies this requirement once it is multiplied by
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">forecast_scale</span></code>.</p>
<p>With a rank-based alpha, calibration reduces to choosing the scale. If
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">forecast_scale</span></code> is too small, transaction costs and risk penalties may dominate the
signal and the optimizer will keep positions close to their starting weights. If it is
too large, the optimizer may overtrade the ranked signal. The <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">calibration_summary</span></code>,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">portfolio_summary</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">decay_summary</span></code> diagnostics help choose a scale that is
consistent with realized target returns and the expected holding horizon.</p>
</section>
</section>
<section id="portfolio-construction">
<span id="factor-model-portfolio-construction"></span><h2>Portfolio Construction<a class="headerlink" href="#portfolio-construction" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h2>
<p>The factor model is a prior estimator and can be passed to any skfolio optimizer
through the <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">prior_estimator</span></code> parameter. The optimizer fits the
prior internally, then consumes its expected returns, covariance and scenarios.
The <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">characteristics</span></code> panel is forwarded to the prior through scikit-learn
metadata routing.</p>
<p>This section builds two portfolios on the fitted model: a factor-constrained
portfolio that trades factor premia through explicit exposure targets, and a
factor-neutral portfolio built from the orthogonal alpha.</p>
<section id="factor-constrained-portfolio">
<h3>Factor-Constrained Portfolio<a class="headerlink" href="#factor-constrained-portfolio" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>The example below builds a dollar-neutral long-short portfolio with positive
momentum and profitability exposures, together with a negative exposure to the
non-linear size factor. The exposure levels are chosen for illustration so their
effects remain clear in the later attribution analysis.</p>
<p>The -2.0 target for <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">non_linear_size</span></code> shows how factor constraints can express
more complex patterns than a simple large-versus-small tilt. The factor is built
as the cube of the size exposure and neutralized against size, which produces
the following approximate tilts:</p>
<div class="pst-scrollable-table-container"><table class="table">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 66.7%" />
<col style="width: 33.3%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr class="row-odd"><th class="head"><p>Size region</p></th>
<th class="head"><p>Portfolio tilt</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr class="row-even"><td><p>Very small</p></td>
<td><p>Long</p></td>
</tr>
<tr class="row-odd"><td><p>Moderately small</p></td>
<td><p>Short</p></td>
</tr>
<tr class="row-even"><td><p>Moderately large</p></td>
<td><p>Long</p></td>
</tr>
<tr class="row-odd"><td><p>Very large</p></td>
<td><p>Short</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sklearn</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">set_config</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">RiskMeasure</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.optimization</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">MeanRisk</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ObjectiveFunction</span>

<span class="n">set_config</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">enable_metadata_routing</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">X</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_dataframe</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">fields</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"returns"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">industry_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fields</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"industry"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">levels</span>

<span class="n">mvo</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">MeanRisk</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">objective_function</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ObjectiveFunction</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">MAXIMIZE_RATIO</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">risk_measure</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">RiskMeasure</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">VARIANCE</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">prior_estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">model</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># factor model as prior</span>
    <span class="n">max_weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.05</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># limit individual positions to 5%</span>
    <span class="n">min_weights</span><span class="o">=-</span><span class="mf">0.05</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># allow short positions and limit to -5%</span>
    <span class="n">budget</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.0</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># dollar neutral</span>
    <span class="n">max_long</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">1.0</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># 100% long and 100% short: 200% gross exposure</span>
    <span class="n">transaction_costs</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.001</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">month</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># 10 bps amortized over one month</span>
    <span class="n">fallback</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"previous_weights"</span><span class="p">,</span>  <span class="c1"># keep last valid weights if a fit fails</span>
    <span class="n">linear_constraints</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">[</span>
        <span class="s2">"momentum &gt;= 1.0"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"profitability == 1.0"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"non_linear_size == -2.0"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="c1"># Exact neutrality</span>
        <span class="s2">"beta == 0"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"size == 0"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"volatility == 0"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="c1"># Small bands on the remaining styles</span>
        <span class="s2">"growth &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"growth &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"investment &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"investment &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"value &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"value &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"liquidity &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"liquidity &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"earnings_yield &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"earnings_yield &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"leverage &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"leverage &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="s2">"dividend_yield &lt;= 0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"dividend_yield &gt;= -0.05"</span><span class="p">,</span>

        <span class="c1"># Industry neutrality</span>
        <span class="o">*</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> == 0.0"</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">industry_names</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="p">],</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">weights_</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="c1"># Factor model fitted inside the optimizer, reused below for attribution</span>
<span class="n">factor_model</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">prior_estimator_</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">factor_model_</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>Here the full coverage universe serves as the investment universe: <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">X</span></code> contains
the returns of every asset in the panel. The factor model fitted inside the optimizer is
available through <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">mvo.prior_estimator_.factor_model_</span></code> and is reused in the
<a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-attribution"><span class="std std-ref">Attribution</span></a> section. A separate factor model
can also be fitted for attribution only.</p>
<p>The portfolio is dollar neutral with <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">budget=0.0</span></code> and limited to 100% long
exposure with <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">max_long=1.0</span></code>. Dollar neutrality implies an equally sized short
position, so the maximum gross exposure is 200% (100% long plus 100% short).</p>
<p>Individual positions are limited to <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\pm 5\%\)</span>. Transaction costs follow the
skfolio convention: a linear cost per unit traded, deducted from the portfolio
expected return, which is expressed per observation period (here daily). A
transaction cost is paid once per rebalancing while a position earns its return
on every period it is held, so the 10 basis points are amortized over the
one-month expected holding period to convert them to a daily cost,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">0.001</span> <span class="pre">/</span> <span class="pre">month</span></code> (see
<a class="reference internal" href="data_preparation.html#periodicity-convention"><span class="std std-ref">Periodicity Convention</span></a>). Market
impact and borrow costs can be added through the optimizer’s <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">add_objective</span></code> and
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">add_constraints</span></code> parameters, with native support planned for a future release.</p>
<p>The portfolio targets three equity styles: momentum, profitability and non-linear size.
The beta, size and volatility exposures are set to zero. The remaining styles are
constrained within <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\pm 0.05\)</span>, and industry exposures are neutralized. In
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">linear_constraints</span></code>, an expression on a factor name (e.g. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">&quot;momentum</span> <span class="pre">&gt;=</span> <span class="pre">1.0&quot;</span></code>)
applies to the portfolio exposure to that factor, while an expression on a family
name (e.g. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">&quot;style</span> <span class="pre">&lt;=</span> <span class="pre">0.5&quot;</span></code>) applies to the sum of exposures over the family’s
factors. Industry neutrality therefore uses one constraint per industry
factor rather than a single <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">&quot;industry</span> <span class="pre">==</span> <span class="pre">0&quot;</span></code> constraint, which would only force
industry exposures to offset each other. No explicit
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">&quot;market</span> <span class="pre">==</span> <span class="pre">0&quot;</span></code> constraint is needed because <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">budget=0.0</span></code> already sets the
global factor exposure to zero.</p>
<p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">fallback=&quot;previous_weights&quot;</span></code> keeps the latest valid allocation when a
rebalancing problem is infeasible, for example on dates where strict constraints
cannot be satisfied. Fallback estimators and the fallback audit trail are covered
in <a class="reference internal" href="../auto_examples/mean_risk/plot_17_failure_and_fallbacks.html#sphx-glr-auto-examples-mean-risk-plot-17-failure-and-fallbacks-py"><span class="std std-ref">Failure and Fallbacks</span></a>.</p>
<p>The portfolio is evaluated using monthly walk-forward rebalancing:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.model_selection</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span>

<span class="c1"># Two years plus one month of observations warm up the model</span>
<span class="c1"># before the first rebalancing</span>
<span class="n">warmup_size</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">252</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">2</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">21</span>

<span class="n">mpp</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">mvo</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">X</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">test_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">month</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">params</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"characteristics"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">n_fallback_portfolios</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">summary</span><span class="p">())</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">annualized_sharpe_ratio</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_cumulative_returns</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/mpp_cumulative_returns.webp"
       alt="Cumulative out-of-sample return of the monthly rebalanced factor-constrained portfolio"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_composition</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/mpp_composition.webp"
       alt="Long and short asset weights of the factor-constrained portfolio across monthly rebalancing dates"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_predict</span></code> returns a <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.html#skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio" title="skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">MultiPeriodPortfolio</span></code></a>, one
portfolio per rebalancing. The <a class="reference internal" href="portfolio.html#portfolio"><span class="std std-ref">Portfolio</span></a> user guide covers the
portfolio objects and their analytics (e.g. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_long_short_exposure</span></code>,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_contribution</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">summary</span></code>). The two-year <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">warmup_size</span></code> covers the stacked
descriptor and estimator warmups (see <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-warmup"><span class="std std-ref">Warmup Periods</span></a>). The out-of-sample portfolio achieves an annualized
Sharpe ratio of 0.91.</p>
<p>The optimizer consumes the factor model with the following conventions:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>For variance-based risk measures, the optimizer consumes the covariance square
root described in the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-asset-covariance-forecast"><span class="std std-ref">Asset Covariance Forecast</span></a> section,
operating in the factor space instead of forming the dense asset covariance.</p></li>
<li><p>For scenario-based risk measures (e.g. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">RiskMeasure.CVAR</span></code>), the optimizer consumes
the asset return scenarios from <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">return_distribution_.returns</span></code>, which combine
factor and idiosyncratic components. With <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-online-learning"><span class="std std-ref">online learning</span></a>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">max_history</span></code> keeps the
scenarios on a rolling window.</p></li>
<li><p>Assets that are not investable at the current date (e.g. delisted, in warmup)
carry NaN moments. The optimizer solves on the investable subset and assigns them
zero weight, as described in the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-input-data"><span class="std std-ref">Input Data</span></a> section.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Robust optimization in the orthogonal space is configured at the optimizer level,
following the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-orthogonal-space-regularization"><span class="std std-ref">Orthogonal Space Regularization</span></a> section:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.uncertainty_set</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">OrthogonalCovarianceUncertaintySet</span>

<span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set_params</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">covariance_uncertainty_set_estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">OrthogonalCovarianceUncertaintySet</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">radius</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">1.0</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>This example trades factor premia and does not use an alpha forecast. The
factor-neutral case is covered in the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-factor-neutral-alpha-portfolio"><span class="std std-ref">Factor-Neutral Alpha Portfolio</span></a> section below.</p>
<p>Walk-forward evaluation and hyperparameter tuning of the full
optimization-plus-prior pipeline are covered in the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-walk-forward-evaluation"><span class="std std-ref">Walk-Forward Evaluation</span></a> and <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-hyper-parameter-tuning"><span class="std std-ref">Hyperparameter Tuning</span></a> sections.</p>
<div class="admonition note">
<p class="admonition-title">Note</p>
<p>When the research focus is the optimization itself and the factor model is
fixed, refitting the prior at each iteration can dominate the runtime on
large universes. The factor model outputs can be precomputed over the chosen
observations, stored in a database or local cache, and served back by a custom
prior estimator that reads them for the requested observation range. Native
factor model caching is planned for a future release.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="factor-neutral-alpha-portfolio">
<span id="factor-model-factor-neutral-alpha-portfolio"></span><h3>Factor-Neutral Alpha Portfolio<a class="headerlink" href="#factor-neutral-alpha-portfolio" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>The previous example earns its return from factor premia through explicit
exposure targets. This portfolio keeps factor exposures close to zero and uses the
orthogonal alpha as its modeled expected return, an approach common in statistical
arbitrage. The orthogonal alpha is the residual of the alpha forecast after
projection onto the factor exposure space.</p>
<div class="admonition note">
<p class="admonition-title">Note</p>
<p>A factor-neutral portfolio has zero exposure to every factor, so its modeled
expected return reduces to the orthogonal alpha. Without an alpha estimator,
the orthogonal alpha is zero and the optimal allocation is the zero portfolio.</p>
</div>
<p>The validated alpha estimator from the <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-alpha"><span class="std std-ref">Alpha Estimators</span></a> section is attached to the factor model, and a
dollar-neutral long-short portfolio is optimized with weekly rebalancing. Style
exposures are bounded within <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\pm 0.05\)</span>, industry exposures are set to
zero, and individual positions are limited to <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\pm 3\%\)</span>. Because
factor-neutral strategies typically run at higher gross leverage, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">max_long</span></code> is raised to
3.0, allowing up to 600% gross exposure. The optimization objective is a mean-variance
utility, balancing the alpha forecast against risk and transaction costs:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.model_selection</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">RiskMeasure</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.optimization</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">MeanRisk</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ObjectiveFunction</span>

<span class="n">week</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">5</span>
<span class="n">model</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set_params</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">alpha_estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">alpha_estimator</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">X</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_dataframe</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">fields</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"returns"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">industry_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fields</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"industry"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">levels</span>
<span class="n">style_factors</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span>
    <span class="s2">"beta"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"momentum"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"profitability"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"non_linear_size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"volatility"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="s2">"growth"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"investment"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"value"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"liquidity"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"earnings_yield"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="s2">"leverage"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"dividend_yield"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">mvo</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">MeanRisk</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">objective_function</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ObjectiveFunction</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">MAXIMIZE_UTILITY</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">risk_measure</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">RiskMeasure</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">VARIANCE</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">risk_aversion</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">prior_estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">model</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">max_weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.03</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">min_weights</span><span class="o">=-</span><span class="mf">0.03</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">budget</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">max_long</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">3.0</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">transaction_costs</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.001</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">week</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">fallback</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"previous_weights"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">linear_constraints</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">[</span>
        <span class="o">*</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> &lt;= 0.05"</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">style_factors</span><span class="p">],</span>
        <span class="o">*</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> &gt;= -0.05"</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">style_factors</span><span class="p">],</span>
        <span class="o">*</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> == 0"</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">industry_names</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="p">],</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">warmup_size</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">2</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">252</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">21</span>
<span class="n">mpp</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">mvo</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">X</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">test_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">week</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">params</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"characteristics"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="n">entry_rebalancing_params</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"transaction_costs"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mf">0.0</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">annualized_sharpe_ratio</span><span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">entry_rebalancing_params</span></code> applies estimator parameters only while constructing
the first portfolio, which starts from cash while later portfolios rebalance
from the previous weights. Setting <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">transaction_costs=0.0</span></code> at entry avoids
charging costs on the full initial ramp-up from cash, letting the first
rebalancing use the desired allocation directly, up to the 600% gross-exposure
limit, instead of building exposure over several rebalancings. The regular
parameters are then restored for subsequent updates. At
this level of gross and short exposure, borrow costs and market impact become
material. They can be added through the optimizer’s <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">add_objective</span></code> and
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">add_constraints</span></code> parameters, with native support planned for a future release.
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.html#skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.plot_cumulative_returns" title="skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.plot_cumulative_returns"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_cumulative_returns</span></code></a> and
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.html#skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.plot_long_short_exposure" title="skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.plot_long_short_exposure"><code class="xref py py-meth docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_long_short_exposure</span></code></a> display
the resulting path and the long and short books.</p>
<p>Realized attribution verifies that the portfolio behaves as intended:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">realized_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">realized_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">compute_uncertainty</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">compute_asset_breakdowns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">realized_attrib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_return_contrib</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">top_n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">15</span><span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/alpha_realized_return_contrib.webp"
       alt="Annualized realized return contributions of factors and the idiosyncratic component for the factor-neutral alpha portfolio"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><p>Factor contributions are negligible and nearly all of the realized return comes
from the idiosyncratic component, around 6.6% annualized with a narrow 95%
confidence interval. The <a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-attribution"><span class="std std-ref">Attribution</span></a> section
covers the methodology.</p>
</section>
</section>
<section id="evaluation-and-tuning">
<h2>Evaluation and Tuning<a class="headerlink" href="#evaluation-and-tuning" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h2>
<p>A factor model pipeline can be evaluated at three complementary levels:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-regression-diagnostics"><span class="std std-ref">Regression diagnostics</span></a> measure how
well the factor structure explains the cross-section of returns.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-covariance-forecast-evaluation"><span class="std std-ref">Covariance forecast evaluation</span></a> tests the out-of-sample calibration of the risk forecasts.</p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-walk-forward-evaluation"><span class="std std-ref">Walk-forward evaluation</span></a> measures the realized portfolio outcomes of
the full pipeline, including the optimizer.</p></li>
</ul>
<section id="warmup-periods">
<span id="factor-model-warmup"></span><h3>Warmup Periods<a class="headerlink" href="#warmup-periods" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>Walk-forward evaluation starts with a warmup period. The <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">warmup_size</span></code> observations
are fitted before the first prediction. Its minimum value follows from the history
each stage of the pipeline consumes before producing its first output.</p>
<p>Rolling and exponentially weighted descriptors (e.g.
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.descriptor.RollingMomentum.html#skfolio.descriptor.RollingMomentum" title="skfolio.descriptor.RollingMomentum"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">RollingMomentum</span></code></a>,
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.descriptor.EWMarketBeta.html#skfolio.descriptor.EWMarketBeta" title="skfolio.descriptor.EWMarketBeta"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">EWMarketBeta</span></code></a>) return NaN during their warmup, and
the cross-sectional regression requires finite exposures, so the longest
descriptor warmup determines the first observation of the factor return
history. The estimators consuming the factor and idiosyncratic return series
add their own warmup on top: the factor prior (covariance and expected returns)
and the idiosyncratic variance estimator. These warmup periods are cumulative.
For example, with one year of descriptor warmup and one year of covariance warmup,
the first usable forecast arrives after about two years of data. The walk-forward
examples in this guide use <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">warmup_size</span> <span class="pre">=</span> <span class="pre">2</span> <span class="pre">*</span> <span class="pre">252</span> <span class="pre">+</span> <span class="pre">21</span></code> for this reason.</p>
<p>Alpha estimators also have warmup periods from their own descriptors, but these
run concurrently with the model’s warmup rather than after it. The model passes
the full <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.containers.AssetPanel.html#skfolio.containers.AssetPanel" title="skfolio.containers.AssetPanel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AssetPanel</span></code></a>, enriched with idiosyncratic
returns, idiosyncratic variances and exposures that keep their leading warmup
NaN values, instead of truncating the panel.</p>
</section>
<section id="walk-forward-evaluation">
<span id="factor-model-walk-forward-evaluation"></span><h3>Walk-Forward Evaluation<a class="headerlink" href="#walk-forward-evaluation" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p><a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.model_selection.online_predict.html#skfolio.model_selection.online_predict" title="skfolio.model_selection.online_predict"><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_predict</span></code></a> simulates the strategy through
time by updating a single stateful estimator with <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">partial_fit</span></code> and predicting
on each subsequent test window:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.model_selection</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span>

<span class="n">portfolio</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">online_predict</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">optimization</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">X</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">2</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">252</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">test_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">params</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"characteristics"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">},</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">portfolio</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">summary</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>Unlike <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.model_selection.cross_val_predict.html#skfolio.model_selection.cross_val_predict" title="skfolio.model_selection.cross_val_predict"><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">cross_val_predict</span></code></a>, which clones and refits
the estimator on each fold, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_predict</span></code> updates a single stateful estimator
and carries its state forward, making long walk-forward backtests practical for
models of this size. The result is a multi-period portfolio with the usual
skfolio analytics (summary, plots, risk measures).
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.model_selection.online_score.html#skfolio.model_selection.online_score" title="skfolio.model_selection.online_score"><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_score</span></code></a> follows the same pattern.</p>
</section>
<section id="hyperparameter-tuning">
<span id="factor-model-hyper-parameter-tuning"></span><h3>Hyperparameter Tuning<a class="headerlink" href="#hyperparameter-tuning" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>All model parameters, from descriptor half-lives to weighting powers, shrinkages
and thresholds, follow the scikit-learn convention and can be tuned with
standard model-selection utilities. For walk-forward tuning,
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.model_selection.OnlineGridSearch.html#skfolio.model_selection.OnlineGridSearch" title="skfolio.model_selection.OnlineGridSearch"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">OnlineGridSearch</span></code></a> and
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.model_selection.OnlineRandomizedSearch.html#skfolio.model_selection.OnlineRandomizedSearch" title="skfolio.model_selection.OnlineRandomizedSearch"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">OnlineRandomizedSearch</span></code></a> evaluate each parameter
combination in a single walk-forward pass using <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">partial_fit</span></code>, instead of refitting
the estimator on every fold:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.model_selection</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">OnlineGridSearch</span>

<span class="n">search</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">OnlineGridSearch</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">optimization</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">param_grid</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="s2">"prior_estimator__inv_idio_variance_weight_shrinkage"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mf">0.0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">0.5</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">1.0</span><span class="p">],</span>
        <span class="s2">"prior_estimator__exposure_lag"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">2</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">252</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">test_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">search</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">search</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">best_params_</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>Scoring can target two levels. Portfolio-level scores (e.g. ratio measures)
evaluate the full pipeline including the optimizer, as in the example above.
Covariance losses from <a class="reference internal" href="../api.html#module-skfolio.metrics" title="skfolio.metrics"><code class="xref py py-mod docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">skfolio.metrics</span></code></a> evaluate the risk model itself
with the factor model being passed directly as the search estimator, without
an optimizer, and scored on its covariance forecast. In <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">make_scorer</span></code>,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">response_method=None</span></code> indicates a non-predictor estimator and
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">greater_is_better=False</span></code> a loss to minimize:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.metrics</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">make_scorer</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">portfolio_variance_qlike_loss</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">skfolio.model_selection</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">OnlineGridSearch</span>

<span class="n">qlike_scorer</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">make_scorer</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">portfolio_variance_qlike_loss</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">greater_is_better</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">response_method</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">None</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">search</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">OnlineGridSearch</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">estimator</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">model</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">param_grid</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="s2">"factor_prior_estimator__covariance_estimator__regime_half_life"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">10</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">63</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="p">},</span>
    <span class="n">scoring</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">qlike_scorer</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">warmup_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">2</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">252</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">test_size</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">21</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">search</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">search</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">best_params_</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>The same workflow is shown on a covariance estimator in
<a class="reference internal" href="../auto_examples/online_learning/plot_2_online_hyperparameter_tuning.html#sphx-glr-auto-examples-online-learning-plot-2-online-hyperparameter-tuning-py"><span class="std std-ref">Online Covariance Hyperparameter Tuning</span></a>.</p>
</section>
</section>
<section id="attribution">
<span id="factor-model-attribution"></span><h2>Attribution<a class="headerlink" href="#attribution" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h2>
<p>Attribution decomposes portfolio risk and return into the contributions of
individual factors, factor families, the idiosyncratic component and,
optionally, individual assets. Applied ex ante, it shows where the forecast
risk and expected return come from. Applied ex post, it shows which factors
delivered the realized performance, with standard errors that separate genuine
contributions from estimation noise.</p>
<p>Attribution is accessed from a <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.portfolio.Portfolio.html#skfolio.portfolio.Portfolio" title="skfolio.portfolio.Portfolio"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">Portfolio</span></code></a> or
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio.html#skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio" title="skfolio.portfolio.MultiPeriodPortfolio"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">MultiPeriodPortfolio</span></code></a>, which supplies the weights
and portfolio returns. Three methods take the fitted factor model as argument:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">predicted_attribution</span></code> computes ex-ante attribution from the fitted loading
matrix, factor covariance and idiosyncratic covariance. The volatility forecast
is decomposed using the exposure-volatility-correlation framework
(<span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(x\)</span>-<span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\sigma\)</span>-<span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\rho\)</span>) and, when expected factor returns are
available, expected return is decomposed into factor-spanned and factor-orthogonal
components.</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">realized_attribution</span></code> computes ex-post attribution from the realized factor
returns, exposures and idiosyncratic returns.</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">rolling_realized_attribution</span></code> runs the realized attribution over rolling
windows, showing how contributions evolve through time.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>The same methods are available at a lower level on the fitted
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.prior.FactorModel.html#skfolio.prior.FactorModel" title="skfolio.prior.FactorModel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">FactorModel</span></code></a>, taking <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">weights</span></code> (a single vector or a
time-varying array) and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">portfolio_returns</span></code> explicitly.</p>
<p>All three return an <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.attribution.Attribution.html#skfolio.attribution.Attribution" title="skfolio.attribution.Attribution"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">Attribution</span></code></a> object
with the same structure:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">systematic</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">idio</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">total</span></code>: component-level breakdowns with volatility,
volatility contribution, share of total variance, return and correlation with
the portfolio. Realized attribution adds <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">unattributed</span></code>, the difference between
observed portfolio returns and model-attributed returns (transaction costs,
fees, cash and intra-period trading).</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">factors</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">families</span></code>: per-factor breakdowns with exposures, standalone
statistics and contributions, with the same information aggregated by factor
family.</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">assets</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">asset_by_factor_contrib</span></code>: the per-asset systematic/idiosyncratic
decomposition and, optionally, the full asset-by-factor contribution matrix.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Realized attribution supports uncertainty estimates (<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">compute_uncertainty=True</span></code>,
the default). Using the stored regression weights and idiosyncratic variances, it
computes standard errors on the factor and idiosyncratic return contributions,
exposed as <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">mu_contrib_uncertainty</span></code> in the factor breakdown.</p>
<p>Results are available as DataFrames through <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">summary_df</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">families_df</span></code> and
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">factors_df</span></code>, and as plots through <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_exposure</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_vol_contrib</span></code>,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_return_contrib</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">plot_return_vs_vol_contrib</span></code>. Rolling attributions
carry an <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">observations</span></code> axis and can be indexed (<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">attribution[i]</span></code>) to retrieve
the attribution of a single window.</p>
<p>Return contributions are reported directly in additive return units. Risk
contributions are additionally normalized as shares of total variance, the
standard scale for comparing risk attribution across portfolios and periods.</p>
<p>The figures below use the constrained mean-variance portfolio from the
<a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-portfolio-construction"><span class="std std-ref">Portfolio Construction</span></a> section. That
portfolio is dollar neutral and imposes three main style constraints:
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">momentum</span> <span class="pre">&gt;=</span> <span class="pre">1.0</span></code>, <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">profitability</span> <span class="pre">==</span> <span class="pre">1.0</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">non_linear_size</span> <span class="pre">==</span> <span class="pre">-2.0</span></code>.
It also sets beta, size, volatility and industry exposures to zero and allows
only small exposures to the remaining style factors.</p>
<section id="ex-ante-attribution">
<h3>Ex-Ante Attribution<a class="headerlink" href="#ex-ante-attribution" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>Ex-ante attribution decomposes the risk and expected-return forecasts of the
optimized portfolio:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="c1"># Access via the Portfolio or MultiPeriodPortfolio API</span>
<span class="n">portfolio</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">predict</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">X</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">predicted_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">portfolio</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">predicted_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="c1"># Equivalent access via the FactorModel API, passing the weights explicitly</span>
<span class="n">predicted_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">predicted_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">mvo</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">weights_</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">predicted_attrib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">summary_df</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>

</div><p>The predicted annualized volatility of 17.5% splits into a 16.0% systematic
volatility contribution and a 1.5% idiosyncratic volatility contribution. Because
each volatility contribution is the corresponding variance contribution divided
by total volatility, dividing again by total volatility gives the variance share:
91.6% systematic and 8.4% idiosyncratic. The expected return is entirely
systematic: no alpha estimator is attached, so the model forecasts no return in
the orthogonal space.</p>

<section id="ex-post-attribution">
<h3>Ex-Post Attribution<a class="headerlink" href="#ex-post-attribution" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>Ex-post attribution decomposes the realized performance of the walk-forward
portfolio, whose weights vary through time:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="c1"># Access via the Portfolio or MultiPeriodPortfolio API</span>
<span class="n">realized_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">realized_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="c1"># Equivalent access via the FactorModel API, passing the time-varying</span>
<span class="c1"># weights and the realized portfolio returns explicitly</span>
<span class="n">realized_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">factor_model</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">realized_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">weights</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="n">portfolio_returns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">portfolio_returns</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">realized_attrib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">summary_df</span><span class="p">()</span>
</pre></div>
</div>

<section id="rolling-attribution">
<h3>Rolling Attribution<a class="headerlink" href="#rolling-attribution" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>Rolling attribution repeats the realized attribution over rolling windows,
showing how contributions evolve through time:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">rolling_realized_attrib</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mpp</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">rolling_realized_attribution</span><span class="p">(</span>

    <span class="n">compute_asset_breakdowns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">rolling_realized_attrib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">summary_df</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">8</span><span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
