      <th rowspan="4" valign="top">2015-11-09</th>
      <th>Systematic</th>
      <td>7.12%</td>
      <td>80.97%</td>
      <td>30.77% ± 11.05%</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <th>Idiosyncratic</th>
      <td>1.67%</td>
      <td>19.04%</td>
      <td>0.08% ± 11.05%</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <th>Unattributed</th>
      <td>-0.00%</td>
      <td>-0.00%</td>
      <td>-0.64%</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <th>Total</th>
      <td>8.79%</td>
      <td>100.00%</td>
      <td>30.21%</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
</table>
</div><p>The summary carries one component breakdown per window, dated at the window end.</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">rolling_realized_attrib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot_exposure</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">top_n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">15</span><span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<figure class="factor-model-figure">
  <img src="../_static/factor_model/plots/attribution_rolling_realized_exposure.webp"
       alt="Rolling factor exposures of the portfolio throughout the backtest"
       width="1100" height="616"
       loading="lazy" decoding="async"
       style="display:block;width:100%;height:auto;">
</figure><p>The constraints hold throughout the backtest: profitability stays at its 1.0
target, non-linear size at -2.0, and momentum near its 1.0 floor, rising above
it when the forecast premium strengthens (e.g. in 2018 and from late 2025).
Indexing the rolling attribution (<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">rolling_realized_attrib[i]</span></code>) retrieves a
single window with the same plots and DataFrames as above.</p>
</section>
</section>
<section id="model-validation-and-review">
<h2>Model Validation and Review<a class="headerlink" href="#model-validation-and-review" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h2>
<p>Users who wish to review the implementation can start with the statistical
recovery suite in
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tests/test_prior/test_characteristics_factor_model/test_statistical_recovery.py</span></code>.
The tests build synthetic panels from known data-generating processes, fit
<a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.prior.CharacteristicsFactorModel.html#skfolio.prior.CharacteristicsFactorModel" title="skfolio.prior.CharacteristicsFactorModel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">CharacteristicsFactorModel</span></code></a> and verify that the estimated
quantities recover the intended model structure.</p>
<p>The suite covers factor returns, loadings, the covariance identity
<span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(\Sigma = B F B^\top + D\)</span>, idiosyncratic risk, residual orthogonality,
exposure lagging, estimation masks, zero-sum constraints, neutralization,
inverse-idiosyncratic-variance weighting, time-varying market capitalizations,
changing universes and currency factors. These checks support implementation
review alongside the empirical diagnostics and walk-forward evaluation described
in this guide.</p>
</section>
<section id="computational-performance">
<span id="factor-model-performance"></span><h2>Computational Performance<a class="headerlink" href="#computational-performance" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h2>
<p>A production factor model processes large datasets. For example, a coverage
universe of 5,000 assets with 10 years of daily data and 80 characteristic
fields holds over a billion entries. This section describes how the
implementation handles this scale and what to expect in terms of fitting time and
memory usage.</p>
<section id="online-learning">
<span id="factor-model-online-learning"></span><h3>Online Learning<a class="headerlink" href="#online-learning" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>The factor model supports online learning. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">partial_fit</span></code> appends new
observations without refitting the history, and the result is identical to a
batch <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">fit</span></code> on the concatenated data. This is used in three ways:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>An <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.containers.AssetPanel.html#skfolio.containers.AssetPanel" title="skfolio.containers.AssetPanel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AssetPanel</span></code></a> that does not fit in memory is
processed chunk by chunk, with only the current chunk held in memory (see
<a class="reference internal" href="#factor-model-memory-usage"><span class="std std-ref">Memory Usage</span></a>).</p></li>
<li><p>Walk-forward evaluation and hyper-parameter tuning run in a single pass over
the data through the online utilities <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_predict</span></code>,
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">online_covariance_forecast_evaluation</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">OnlineGridSearch</span></code>.</p></li>
<li><p>In production, daily updates only fit the new observation instead of refitting
the full history. Even for large models, this update runs in well under a
second in the benchmarks below.</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Internal state (estimator warmup, exposure and weight lag buffers, constraint
bases) is carried across calls, and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">max_history</span></code> bounds the retained
time-series outputs to a rolling window.</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="n">model</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">fit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">[:</span><span class="n">warmup</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">i</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="nb">range</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">warmup</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="n">model</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">partial_fit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">characteristics</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">i</span> <span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">i</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">])</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>See <a class="reference internal" href="online_learning.html#online-learning"><span class="std std-ref">Online Learning</span></a> for the general framework.</p>
</section>
<section id="benchmarks">
<h3>Benchmarks<a class="headerlink" href="#benchmarks" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>The following fitting times were measured on 10 years of daily data (2,520
observations) on a laptop (Ultra 9 275HX, 24 cores, 32 GB RAM), for two models:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>Model 1: 16 factors (1 global, 10 industries, 5 styles) with default parameters.</p></li>
<li><p>Model 2: the 58-factor model used throughout this guide (1 global, 44 industries, 13 styles
from 29 descriptors), with within-industry scoring, neutralization, zero-sum
constraints, two-pass inverse-idiosyncratic-variance regression weights and the
regime-adjusted covariance estimator.</p></li>
</ul>
<div class="pst-scrollable-table-container"><table class="table">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 30.0%" />
<col style="width: 35.0%" />
<col style="width: 35.0%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr class="row-odd"><th class="head"><p>Assets</p></th>
<th class="head"><p>Model 1</p></th>
<th class="head"><p>Model 2</p></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr class="row-even"><td><p>500</p></td>
<td><p>3 s</p></td>
<td><p>14 s</p></td>
</tr>
<tr class="row-odd"><td><p>1,000</p></td>
<td><p>5 s</p></td>
<td><p>22 s</p></td>
</tr>
<tr class="row-even"><td><p>5,000</p></td>
<td><p>25 s</p></td>
<td><p>87 s</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p>An incremental <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">partial_fit</span></code> on the next observation runs in under a second for both
models.</p>
<p>Achieving this performance relies on two implementation choices. First, the
hot paths are vectorized NumPy operations backed by parallel BLAS kernels.
Second, factor exposures are computed with thread-based parallelism (<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">n_jobs</span></code>),
which avoids copying the panel to worker processes (the computations are
NumPy-dominated and release the GIL, so threads provide effective parallelism
while sharing the panel in memory).</p>
</section>
<section id="memory-usage">
<span id="factor-model-memory-usage"></span><h3>Memory Usage<a class="headerlink" href="#memory-usage" title="Link to this heading">#</a></h3>
<p>By default, all panel data is held in memory for vectorized operations and
thread-based parallelism. A coverage
universe of 5,000 assets with 10 years of daily data (2,500 observations) and 80
characteristic fields holds <span class="math notranslate nohighlight">\(5{,}000 \times 80 \times 2{,}500 = 10^9\)</span> entries,
or 8 GB in float64. This fits on a typical 32 GB machine.</p>
<p>When memory becomes a constraint, three options are available:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>Process the data in chunks with <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">partial_fit</span></code>, keeping only the current batch in
memory and bounding the retained outputs with <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">max_history</span></code>.</p></li>
<li><p>Store selected fields as float32 in the <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.containers.AssetPanel.html#skfolio.containers.AssetPanel" title="skfolio.containers.AssetPanel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AssetPanel</span></code></a>,
halving their footprint.</p></li>
<li><p>Subclass <a class="reference internal" href="../generated/skfolio.containers.AssetPanel.html#skfolio.containers.AssetPanel" title="skfolio.containers.AssetPanel"><code class="xref py py-class docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AssetPanel</span></code></a> to load characteristic fields
lazily and release them once descriptors have consumed them.</p></li>
</ul>
</section>
</section>
